| 22 | ~~**Aurora's writer and aeon's `json.dump` disagree on JSON separators, so every alternation rewrites 100% of `editor_bg_override.json` — invisibly.**~~ — **DISCHARGED BY ITEM 25**, code `0376039` (`src/core/formats/canonical-json.ts`, the §5 canonical-serialization chokepoint) — `--stat`-verified as a code commit and an ancestor of master. **This row carried no delivery marker of any kind until 2026-08-26 and read fully open.** Original booking follows. Measured 2026-08-22 by the item-20 composition probe (`86d9fb1`), as a side effect rather than as its object. Identical semantics (`a == c` is True), **not byte-identical**: Aurora emits **282,867 B** where aeon's `json.dump` emits **407,055 B** for the same document — 30% smaller, both a single line, differing only in the `,`/`:` separators (python defaults to `", "`/`": "`). **Not a correctness bug** and not asserted to be one. What makes it worth booking is the interaction with a hazard this arc already ruled on: the ownership ruling established that `--stat` reads `editor_bg_override.json | 2 +-` on this file *because the JSON is minified*, which is how two authored bands were destroyed for a month with nobody noticing. Format churn means **the first Aurora save rewrites the entire file and still shows as one line**, and so does every subsequent alternation between the two writers. The review discipline that failed once — reading `--stat` and seeing a one-line change — fails identically here, now on a file with a sole writer of record whose whole justification is that the previous arrangement destroyed data quietly. **RESOLVED 2026-08-22, same day, and the option I called most likely was REFUTED by the aeon overseer.** I had offered three readings and flagged the third — *correctly ignored, because a sole writer means no alternation happens* — as the probable answer. It is wrong: **`tools/bg_override_io.py` writes this file via `json.dumps(obj).encode()`**, Python's default separators, which is the other half of the 407,055 vs 282,867 B delta. The document has **two writers with different serializations today**, so alternation is not hypothetical and the sole-writer-of-record ruling governs *authority over content*, not *serialization*. Two distinct things that the phrase 'sole writer' silently merges. **Ruled: canonicalize, don't match.** Matching separators fixes the churn that happened to be measured and leaves **key order** waiting to produce the identical surprise later; a *determined* serialization is the property worth having (agreed separators + `sort_keys`), after which any diff at all is semantic. Pretty-printing was considered and **dropped on their scale argument** — a hundred-thousand-line diff across tile arrays is the same unreviewability in a different costume, and canonical-and-minified already delivers the property that matters: a diff appears only when something semantic changed, which is the actual defense against the month-long silent death. **aeon carries it** into `tools/EFFECTS_CONSUMER_CONTRACT.md` as a serialization clause and changes `bg_override_io.py`; **Aurora adopts on its own schedule** — it costs us a writer-golden re-pin (small, ours, gates nobody), and `sort_keys` reorders the unknown keys we round-trip untouched, which stays semantically faithful but should be said out loud in the posture note so a future reader does not read reordering as editing. **Transition caveat, raised by this lane and the one part that is not about the destination:** adopting canonical form churns the whole file **once** per side, and that one-time churn has exactly the shape of the hazard the clause exists to prevent. **Land the reserialization as its own commit that changes nothing else**, on both sides, with a message saying the diff is format-only — otherwise the first thing the new clause does is hide a real edit. **ADOPTED 2026-08-22 — see item 25**, which discharges this row. Two things in the summary above were superseded by aeon's own amendment before Aurora landed it: the clause is **recursive** (nested band and layer objects sort too, not just the top level), and pretty-print was **not** dropped outright — it was ruled per document class, so the scene files pretty-print and only the tile-array documents minify. The transition caveat this lane raised became contract text, along with a fixture exception this lane raised unprompted. | XS | overseer, 2026-08-22 |

| 23 | ~~**Wave-1 surface 4, part 2 — the BgAnim band model + the one undoable add/remove-band command**~~ — **DELIVERED 2026-08-22**, branch `feat/bganim-band-command` (`0e66a79` the contract extension, `d20c94d` the model + command, `5aa5c35` a vacuity fix). `src/core/formats/bg-override/bg-anim-band.ts` (model, plan, insert/remove), `src/core/editing/bg-override-band.ts` (the command builders), `SetBgOverrideBandCommand` + `S4Level.bgOverride` + both `history.ts` branches. Suite **4289 passed / 3 skipped** (4242 before; +47), `tsc` clean. **Still no UI and no caller** — part 3. **THE PLAN IS THE PARCEL.** Adding a band inserts its phase-0 art as tiles at the front-ward end of the blob, which renumbers every static tile after it and invalidates every layout word that named one; doing any two of those three bakes cleanly and ships corrupt art (TOOL-01). So the unit of work is a **symmetric plan** computed before anything is touched — each layout entry records BOTH its with-band and its without-band word, so `insertBand`/`removeBand` are literal inverses and history's apply and undo are those same two functions with arguments swapped, not two implementations that can drift. **THE RENDERED PICTURE IS THE INVARIANT, not "it still validates"** (the corrupt case validates): image invariance is asserted **cell by cell over all 4096 nametable words** of the real `b0e5a661` document, through a resolver written from aeon's own nametable loop rather than from the code under test, for appends AND for an insert at position 0 (where every index moves). Round trips are **byte-identical through `serializeBgOverride`**, with `serialize(golden) === serialize(golden)` run first as the nondeterminism control. **ONE NEW CONTRACT CONSTANT, extracted not remembered:** `LAYOUT_TILE_INDEX_MASK`, from `inject_editor_bg.py`'s `if word != 0: idx = word & 0x7FF; word = (word & ~0x7FF) | ...`. The half a reader forgets is not the mask but the **ESCAPE** — a word of exactly 0 draws VRAM tile 0 and is **not** a reference to `tiles[0]` — and the corollary: a renumbering that would turn a nonzero attr-free word INTO 0 is **unrepresentable**, so it refuses rather than blanking a drawn cell. Contract hash re-pinned; the drift gate's own coverage assertion forced the export. **REMOVAL WILL NOT SILENTLY DELETE ART:** the real document draws its two bands in **2560 of its 4096 cells**, so removal refuses while any cell names the band, says how many, and takes `blankReferencingCells` as the caller stating it meant to lose that art. (Demoting a band to static art — image-preserving but *not* a byte-identical inverse, so a different operation — is deliberately **not** in this parcel.) **Bar 12 handled at the two copiers this parcel introduces** (the `slot_base` resync and the owned-key rebuild): both are spreads, never field lists, and both are pinned with unknown top-level AND unknown band keys plus the codec's deliberately illegal palette — the hand-enumerated-literal plant on the resync fails with `expected undefined to be 'a tool that does not exist yet'`. **19 red-first plants, each grepped for site uniqueness first** — which mattered again: `history.ts` now carries the `set-bg-override-band` dispatch **twice** (apply and undo are near-identical, differing only in which way the ternary points), and the undo-direction plant had to hit line 295 and not 151. **ONE PLANT CAME BACK GREEN AND THAT IS THE FINDING:** deleting the `word === 0` escape left the whole suite passing, because the row poisoned a cell and then APPENDED, and on an append tile index 0 does not move — the row was vacuous. Re-cut at position 0 it now fails `expected 8 to be +0` (`5aa5c35`). **⚠ STILL OPEN, unchanged from part 1 and untouchable from a background agent:** no writer has yet produced `anims` bytes aeon's gate has judged — composition needs a real save + `python3 tools/inject_editor_bg.py` + a build, which is foreground work. Add is disabled at `>= max`, Remove at `<= min`, both live in between, so **every count 1..8 is reachable** and 8 is a hard cap. **The punchline: 8 is aeon's OWN `MAX_PARALLAX_BANDS` (§2.1)**, round-tripped through empyrean's schema into Aurora's UI and back at them — so their registry is not missing shapes relative to external demand, it is **incomplete relative to its own engine's declared maximum**. Recommended the wider parcel (add 3, 6, 7, 8 and close the range), since adding only `lower8` leaves the identical defect re-armed with the discovering artifact already spent. **Why this is the frame-break argument, not a lucky catch:** `canopy_dusk.json` is shape coverage over the SCHEMA and could enumerate every optional key forever without producing an unlowerable band count, because **the schema does not know band counts are the constraint**. Pressing Add-layer until it refused enumerated over **the app's own vocabulary** and walked straight into it. Two derivations, genuinely different parameters — corroboration, not echo — found for the cost of one command. **DO NOT regenerate this fixture with fewer layers.** It is worth more failing than passing, and re-authoring it to fit the limitation it discovered would destroy the property that discovered it. | aeon's ask, 2026-08-22 |

| 32 | ~~**`CollapsibleSection` swallows its own header-action clicks — every action button in a section header ALSO toggles the section.**~~ — **DONE 2026-08-24**, code `5041f6e` + CDP harness `18bf2fc` (`scratchpad/section-header-action-harness.mjs`, 639 lines); both `--stat`-verified and ancestors of master. Fixed in the COMPONENT, not the call sites: `right` is a slot, so a rule covering today's two buttons is a list to forget to update. The discrimination is on what was CLICKED — walk up from the target to the header and refuse the toggle if an interactive element is on that path; title, chevron and dead space all still toggle. **This row carried no delivery marker until 2026-08-26 and read fully open.** Original booking follows. Found 2026-08-22 by the item-31 harness, not by a test: its first run saw *"Layers (8/8)"* on screen with **zero layer cards under it**, because the 7th (odd) **Add layer** click had collapsed the section it had just filled. `CollapsibleSection.tsx:61` wraps `<PanelHeader right={right}>` in `<div onClick={toggle}>` and `IconButton` does not stop propagation, so the action's click bubbles to the toggle. **Blast radius measured rather than asserted: 3 call sites pass `right=`, of which exactly 2 are interactive** — `EffectsScenePanel`'s **Delete scene** and **Add layer** — while `ProjectSetupTab`'s passes a plain `<span>` and is harmless. Structurally, though, it is every future header action too, which is why the fix belongs in the component and not the call sites. **Both live victims are in the panel this arc just shipped**, so it is a self-inflicted wound on new code. Note what caught it: **a real gesture under CDP, not the node suite** — 4,427 tests cannot see a click bubbling, which is bar 1 earning its place again. | S | item-31 harness, 2026-08-22 |

**DELIVERED 2026-08-24**, merged `8a3bbd7` (fix `5041f6e`, harness `18bf2fc`). The header wrapper's `onClick={toggle}` becomes `onClick={onHeaderClick}`, which refuses the toggle when the click belongs to a control: a new `isHeaderAction(target, header)` walks up from `e.target` **stopping at the header** and returns true if anything on that path matches an interactive tag, ARIA role, `label`, or `contenteditable`. **The walk stops at the header rather than using a bare `closest()`** — `closest` would climb into the section's own ancestors, so a `CollapsibleSection` nested inside anything interactive would get a permanently dead toggle, which is a worse and far less visible bug than the one being fixed. Two alternatives were rejected in the component's own comments: call-site `stopPropagation` (a slot needs a rule, not a list of today's buttons) and wrapping the whole `right` slot (too wide — it would kill the toggle for the plain `<span>` counters, which are part of the generous hit area on purpose).

**Verified on the MERGED tree, both frames, by the overseer** — never on the branch alone. Green: **31/31**. Red, reverting only the one `onClick` and rebuilding: **24/31**, the same seven rows the agent reported, with **4b reproducing the original defect verbatim** — heading *"Layers (8/8)"* over **0 cards, 0 body nodes, body 0px**, against 8 cards / 129px on the fix. Suite **4468 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed** (349 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Two things about the harness worth keeping.** **(a) The load-bearing observable is the PERSISTED PANEL-STATE WRITE, not the DOM.** A section that unmounts and remounts re-reads `loadPanelState()`, so "are the children still there" has a second route back to green; nothing un-writes `localStorage`. Row 5c is the case where only the storage observable exists at all — clicking *Delete scene* unmounts the section, so the DOM question is unanswerable while the stray write survives and waits for the next session. **(b) Assert after EVERY click, never at the end.** In the red frame clicks 1 and 3 fail while **click 2 PASSES**, because the second stray toggle cancels the first — an end-state-only guard would have been green for every even count, which is exactly how this defect hid behind 4,400 tests in the first place.

**⚠ THIS ROW'S OWN BLAST-RADIUS COUNT WAS WRONG, and the correction was re-derived here independently rather than adopted.** It said *3 call sites pass `right=`*. It is **4**: `EffectsScenePanel:214` (Delete scene) and `:282` (Add layer) are the two interactive ones, and **`ProjectSetupTab:248` AND `shell/Explorer.tsx:245`** both pass a plain `<span>` counter. Explorer was the missed site. **The severity claim is unchanged** — still exactly 2 interactive victims, both in the panel this arc shipped. The sharper detail: **`Explorer` is the one call site carrying `collapsedOverride`**, so this row's own blast-radius list contained the live example of the override trap that would have made a guard written against it vacuous.

**Deliberately NOT done, and booked rather than smuggled in:** the header is still keyboard-inaccessible — a `<div onClick>` with no `role`, `tabIndex` or `aria-expanded`, so the toggle is mouse-only and screen readers announce nothing. Pre-existing and orthogonal, and adding `role="button"` would have interacted with the new selector, so it was left alone. **Worth its own row.** Also: the portal branch of `isHeaderAction` is reasoned, not measured — nothing in the tree renders a header action into a portal today, so the harness has no subject for it, and it is flagged rather than faked. | S | item 31's harness |

| 33 | ~~**Unserved bus methods must fail LOUDLY and by name, and the handshake must record what answered**~~ — **DELIVERED 2026-08-22**, branch `fix/aether-unserved-methods` (`9ae3949` the fix + new `src/main/aether/unserved.ts`, `6b0a754` the tests). Cut in response to the suite's cutover to oracle's Rust core: Aurora is the **only outbound Aether client**, both servers resolve the same socket chain, so the client can change implementation with no config change and no signal. **`MethodNotServedError` carries `method`, `code`, `detectedBy`, and BOTH routes are wired** — a pre-check against the advertised list (no round trip, so a failing call never reaches a sequence that pauses the machine on its way down) *and* a `-32601` reply mapped to the same class, which is the advertised-but-unimplemented shape the list cannot see. **All 20 catch sites in `src/main/aether/` enumerated by what CATCHES A BUS CALL** — 11 made loud, 5 already loud, 4 ratified as legitimate (a JSON parse of a wire line; the inbound server; an SSE write to a dead subscriber; and `bridge.ts`'s aeon→classic fall-through, where a failed resolve is genuinely *how the probe learns the listing is not aeon's* — only the unserved arm was split off). A repo-wide grep confirms no outbound bus call sites exist outside that directory. `AetherHandshake` records `serverName`/`serverVersion`/`protocolVersion`/`methods`/`methodCount`/`capabilities`, survives teardown while `hasMethod` goes false, and reaches the store and the agent surface. **Suite 4463 / 3 skipped on the merged tree** (cross-checked by derivation: master 4427 + the branch's 36 new rows = 4463), `tsc` clean. **27 planted violations, 27/27 red, each restored.** **The three poisons that came back GREEN are the parcel's real content, and none was a bad guard:** (1) a row aimed at a branch the up-front `hasMethod` loop makes **unreachable**; (2) a row proving only *"it refused"*, which BOTH routes satisfy, so deleting the pre-check left it green — now pins which route by asserting whether `emulator/status` was called at all; (3) **the wrong observable** — the fixture left `Player_1` resolvable, so the named catch was never entered and the row was measuring a different gate. **Planting could not have revealed (3); only asking whether it measured the right quantity did** (bar 2b). Fixture corrections flagged deliberately: `hasMethod: () => true` in three fakes described a server nobody ships, and one existing row was retargeted off `-32601` because using the unserved code meant it had quietly become a test of the unserved path rather than of what its name says. | M | empyrean cutover ruling, 2026-08-22 |

| 34 | ~~**Settle empyrean's standing claim that Aurora *"still ships Nemesis/Kosinski compression in TS that the engine has removed"*, flagged there as contract drift**~~ — **REFUTED 2026-08-22.** Raised by the empyrean lane as unverified and explicitly not asserted; settled here firsthand. **The literal half is true and the implication is false.** True: Aurora ships `src/core/compress/nemesis.ts` and `src/core/formats/kosinski.ts`, and **aeon uses neither** — 0 files in their tree match kosinski/nemesis, 11 match ZX0 (`engine/compression/zx0_resume.emp`, the `.zx0` act-pool pages). False: that this makes it dead code or drift. **Aurora is a TWO-ENGINE editor and the codecs are load-bearing on both sides, for different reasons.** (a) The **classic S1/S2/S3K spine** — which §2.6 calls the *motivating* spine, the one engine where a whole level can be authored end-to-end today — is Kosinski/Nemesis natively: `classicLevelStore`, `classicObjectArtStore`, `s1.ts`, `s2.ts`, `s3k.ts`, `s1-art-write.ts`, plus goldens. (b) An **aeon-facing import bridge**: `chunk-library-import.ts` decompresses Kosinski chunk/block/art files, and its UI `AeonChunkActions` is mounted as `HeaderExtra` on the **aeon** chunk grid (`chunk-grid-aeon.ts:174`) — i.e. the documented donor-data workflow, which is how art reaches aeon in the first place. **What is genuinely absent is the thing that would BE drift:** aeon's project load/save path imports neither codec (grep over `src/core/project/aeon/` — **exit 1, 0 hits, directory confirmed to exist**, because an empty result from a failed command is the trap here). So no aeon *runtime or persistence* path depends on them; the engine's format is ZX0 and always reads as ZX0. **Recommend empyrean drop or re-word the claim** — as written it would license deleting the classic spine's compression. | S | empyrean, 2026-08-22 |

| 35 | ~~**`v_factor`'s type was wrong in the CONTRACT, so Aurora's UI offered values no engine could consume**~~ — **DELIVERED 2026-08-23, branch `fix/v-factor-retype`.** Found by the aeon lane against Aurora's **writer-originated** fixture (item 31); ruled by empyrean `45136a1`; applied by empyrean `a32bcb03`. **Schema re-vendored BY EXTRACTION** (`git -C ../empyrean show a32bcb03:contract/schema/… > <vendored>`), never retyped — hashes `cab3ca58…`, and empyrean's **tip** still carries that blob, so the drift ritual says the contract has not moved past what we pinned. `v_factor`/`v_factor_fg` lose the `$ref` to `$defs/factor` and become `{integer, 0..15}`; **`$defs/factor` is untouched** and still governs `fa`, `fb`, `curve.to`. Code anchor `44cad84` (re-vendor + re-pin), `7eda34f` (model + default), `427cbd1` (UI), `e4705d9` (fixture re-origination). **THE `2d7a9fee` HIT COUNT WAS 7 FILES / 10 LINES, NOT 4** — the four code/test sites the brief predicted (`effects-schema-drift.test.ts` ×2 lines, `scene.ts`, `effects-scene-golden.test.ts`, `effects-scene.test.ts`), **plus three doc sites nobody counted**: `docs/OVERSEER.md:281` (bar 16's live citation — **updated**, and it gains the other half of its own lesson: when the pin moves, extract the blob, never hand-edit the vendored copy), and `docs/ROADMAP.md` items 11 and 12 plus `docs/decisions.jsonl` (**deliberately left**: those are historical records of what was true when they were written, and rewriting them would falsify the record). **BLAST RADIUS WAS WIDER THAN BOOKED, in one specific way worth remembering:** the row said `canopy_dusk.json` carried a bad `v_factor_fg` and that `writer_session_ojz.json`'s `fa: "FACTOR_0"` was legal. Both true. But **`canopy_dusk`'s own `v_factor` (`"FACTOR_1_2"`) and `writer_session_ojz`'s `v_factor` (`"FACTOR_3_4"`) were ALSO live defects** — the enumeration had counted the *reserved* field and the *legal* field and skipped the one in between. Bar 12's lesson in miniature: enumerate what TOUCHES the field, not what the prose already names. **The writer fixture was RE-ORIGINATED, not edited** — its provenance forbids hand-editing in as many words, so the CDP session was re-run against the rebuilt app (25/25, up from 22/22) and **exactly one line differs** across the two runs, every layer and factor spelling byte-identical. New identity blob `2ee83f89…`. R7 re-derived: the layer count typed into the real spinner, deliberately **not** the field's `max`, because `max` is also the new-scene default and a fixture carrying it would prove the control moved nothing. **Part E is verified on the running app, not on node** — harness rows 6d/6e read the affordance off the live DOM (an `input[type=number]`, min 0, **no `<select>` at `v_factor` at all**, nothing offering a `FACTOR_*` option) and 7e proves the emitted value is an integer inside that range. **Default migrated `FACTOR_0` → 15, and derived TWICE so the halves cannot drift**: `EFFECTS_V_FACTOR_BOUNDS = boundsAt('properties','v_factor')` — pointedly not read through `$defs/factor`, so a future amendment that `$ref`s the field again fails the module's import — and `EFFECTS_V_FACTOR_LOCK` is that range's `maximum`, with a module-load assertion that the schema's own description still names *that* number as its LOCK SENTINEL. **Red-first, all five poisons:** (a) revert `v_factor` to `$ref: #/$defs/factor` → *"effects scene schema properties.v_factor has no numeric minimum/maximum"*, **20 rows red across four files including the drift gate**; (b) restore `v_factor: 'FACTOR_0'` at `newEffectsScene` → *"refusing to write scene \"canopy_dusk\": /v_factor: expected integer, got string"*, 3 rows red; (c) set `maximum` to 14 leaving the description at 15 → the module-load coupling assertion fires by name; (d) re-pin the drift gate to `2d7a9fee` → red; (e) **the alternative-green-path check** — strip ONLY `"type": "integer"` from the schema (leaving `minimum`/`maximum`) and the FACTOR_* regression row goes red on its own, which rules out the row being held green by some *other* refusal in the same `serialize` call. **Matcher hardened before believing it** (bar 2c): the bounds row first matched `/v_factor/`, which the **`required`** error also satisfies — a schema that DROPPED the field would have reported it green. Re-cut against the bounds rule's own wording, with the numbers read out of the schema. **Totals: 4468 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed (350 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean.** **STILL OPEN, deliberately, and both re-confirmed rather than inherited:** `v_offset` is **NOT RULED** — schema `integer` vs aeon's `u16`, so a negative offset is schema-legal and engine-illegal, and since a negative vertical offset is physically meaningful **this may be the engine that is wrong**; our fixtures carry `v_offset: -8` and it stays. A `v_factor` **rename** stays unruled — it reads like `fa`/`fb`, which is almost certainly how the wrong `$ref` got written, but a rename is a wire break needing its own pricing. ⚠ **NOBODY HAS RUN THE ENGINE HALF.** The 255-folds-to-a-sign-fill mechanism is derived from the ISA and from reading `parallax.emp`, and this parcel did not change that: **no emulator was touched** (a standing invariant for background lanes here). What is now proven is that Aurora writes a value the contract accepts and the UI cannot spell a FACTOR_* name into that field. Whether 15 renders as a lock **in the running engine** is a foreground/aeon-side confirmation, and it is the one thing this row does not claim. **Method note, unchanged and worth keeping:** a fixture derived from the contract CANNOT test the contract — the hand-written shape-coverage fixture is schema-legal by construction and could never reach this class; the real-session artifact found it. | S | aeon/empyrean, 2026-08-22; CR landed 2026-08-23; DELIVERED 2026-08-23 |

Each fixed at its call site with a clamp derived from the same source as its props (`clampFieldValue(field, raw)` in `ObjectInspector`; `clampStaticBase(n, budget.firstPromotableSlot)` in `BgAnimBandPanel`), per item 37's standing decline of a self-clamping `NumberField`. **BOTH PATHS IN THE OLD ROW WERE WRONG and the parcel corrected them**: the inspector is `components/shared/ObjectInspector.tsx`, not `components/inspector/`, and the band field is at ~:650 — **`:311` is a different `firstPromotableSlot` guard**, a `useEffect` that fires only when the document moves, never on a typed value. **The row's characterisation of site 1 was also wrong, and the correction is the interesting part:** the value was NOT reaching the document unclamped — both ports call `clampPatch` — but each does so against a schema it **re-derives in its own commit callback**, not the schema whose `IntField` supplied the displayed props. | 41 | ~~**Effects panel cleanup (owner ask 2026-08-26, relayed by the hub).**~~ — **DELIVERED 2026-08-26**, branch `fix/effects-panel-tidy` (`f99ffa1` the design pass, `b3dcfc6` the harness, `6a32cee` instrument maintenance). Suite **4523 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed**, `tsc` clean, CDP **19/19** at 1680x1050 AND at 1280x800. **HALF THE DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG AND THE MEASUREMENT SAID SO.** The dispatch's structural hypothesis HELD: three of this column's four `variant="list"` sections were splitting a column with nothing to split, and all three sat at EXACTLY the 160px floor, each with its own inner scrollbar, inside a column overflowing by 292px — four scrollbars, three of them 160px tall. The LABEL hypothesis did NOT. Every labelled row in the column already placed its control at exactly **72px**; the widest label text measured **57px** against a 68px floor and nothing overran it. What was ragged is that **four rows carried a SECOND label mid-row** (V center/V offset, Precision/Transition, Cols/Rows, and a hint wedged between From tile's box and its Promote chip) — and a second label sits at whatever x the first control happened to end at, which no shared width can govern. **THE DESIGN.** `effects/column-layout.tsx` declares the column's geometry once for both panels (Field/Row/Hint/Group/Card); `LABEL_W = 64`, derived from a DOM-Range measurement of every rendered label rather than chosen, and 4px NARROWER than before because the label that set the old floor was `#0 world_y` — a layer INDEX folded into a field name, which now titles the card. A band boundary would be placed through level geometry moving at a different rate, and a click becomes ambiguous about which plane it is for — so the authoring affordances and the fidelity pull in opposite directions on one surface. **His own resolution, and it is better than a second window:** let the FG toggle BE the mode switch — foreground off is the authoring view (background only, camera preview per row 69), foreground on is the full game view. The foreground is Plane A with its own factor, plus level geometry, sprites and priority — **none of which Aurora composites today**, and row 69's packet is required to state that absence rather than approximate it. This row is therefore gated on a Plane-A compositing path existing at all, not on a toggle. **Open questions for whoever picks this up:** whether editing affordances are DISABLED with FG on (a watch mode) or merely deprioritised; whether the FG comes from the level tab's own tiles or a separate source; and whether the toggle is per-scene or per-session. ⚠ **AND THE OWNER IMMEDIATELY UNDERCUT HIS OWN RESOLUTION, WHICH IS WHY THIS ROW IS NOT AN APPROVED DESIGN.** His next words: *"the editor would have fg parallax? if so we can't disable it."* **He is right, and it is decisive.** Every scene layer already authors **`fa` — the PLANE A (foreground) factor** — alongside `fb`. A toggle that 'disables the foreground' would therefore be hiding one of the two fields each band carries, which is not a view mode — it is concealing half the document. **So 'FG on = preview mode, FG off = authoring mode' cannot be the shape**, because there is no coherent authoring mode in which the foreground is absent. **What survives:** the tension he named (a composited canvas stops being editable) is real and unresolved; his rejection of a second window still stands; and the honest statement of the problem is now *how do you show Plane A's parallax faithfully on a surface where Plane A's factor is an editable field* — which nobody has answered. **Whoever picks this up starts from that question, not from the toggle.** | L | the owner, 2026-08-27, parked by him |

| 69 | ~~**Aurora had never turned a `FACTOR_*` into a number — it stored them as names or `{s1,s2,op}` triples and there was no decoder anywhere, so nothing on the canvas could say where a band would actually sit.**~~ — **DELIVERED 2026-08-27**, branch `feat/camera-preview`, commit `d1a7a44`, packet `docs/reviews/2026-08-27-camera-preview.md` §1. `src/core/formats/effects/factor-decode.ts` is `Decode_Factor_A` (`parallax.emp:1665`) transcribed TERM FOR TERM, with the proc's closing `neg.w` left off and named separately as `hscrollWord` — a VDP HScroll word is a NEGATED offset, and a sign error here reads as "the art is mirrored" rather than as arithmetic. **THE TRIPLE IS THE DEFINITION AND THE NAME IS A HINT**, and the two are SHOWN to agree: all sixteen published names, driven from `EFFECTS_FACTOR_NAMES` (read out of the vendored schema, not typed), against a fraction the TEST parses from the name — a second independent statement that deliberately does not exist in `src`. **Sixteen agreements, no disagreement.** ⚠ **THE ONE NON-AGREEMENT, and it is not a defect:** the decode is neither `camX*num/den` NOR its floor, because `asr.w` truncates toward -inf PER TERM before the terms combine — at camX 7, `FACTOR_3_4` decodes to **6** while the product is 5.25 and its floor is 5, three different numbers. `factorRatio` exists for LABELS and the agreement table only and says so. ⚠ **THE SENTINEL, AND THE HONEST BOUNDARY:** `s1 == 15` is locked, `s2 == 15` is single-term — and **a decoder that treated 15 as an ordinary shift is NUMERICALLY INVISIBLE for camX in 0..32767**, because `camX >> 15` is 0 and 0 is the identity for both `+` and `-`. What catches it: `factorIsLocked` (structural, any camX); negative camX and camX >= $8000 (`asr.w` sign-fills); and the VERTICAL sentinel, which IS reachable — `((camY - v_center) >> 15) + v_offset` equals `v_offset` while `camY >= v_center` and **`v_offset - 1` the moment it is not**. So the DRAW moved instead — `canvas/tile-lens.ts` is classic's `drawPriority` loop lifted verbatim over a boolean predicate, and **classic's own four geometry tests are what say the move perturbed nothing**. ⚠ **THE ONE REAL DIFFERENCE IS THE WINDOW, and it is not cosmetic:** an aeon section is 256x256 = 65,536 tiles against classic's 1,024-tile chunk, and an act holds up to `MAX_ACT_SECTIONS = 48` — **~3.1M predicate calls per repaint unwindowed**, against ~15.7ms of measured headroom. | 73 | ~~**"Painting collision feels bad... you can't really see what the shape looks like over the art", "these lines aren't really useful to describe direction", "collision angles view is just bad" — three complaints, one subject: how the app depicts a collision shape and its direction.**~~ — **DELIVERED 2026-08-28**, branch `feat/collision-legibility`. **The root cause was a correctness bug, not taste.** Reproducing each call site's angle formula verbatim showed the four surfaces held THREE conventions, and the one an author paints on was a mirror: at angle $20 (45°) the aeon map drew `(0.707, -0.707)` while classic's map and the picker/ghost both drew `(0.707, +0.707)`. Every non-flat angle on the aeon map lay ACROSS the slope it sat on instead of along it. On top of that the mark was **centred** (floating at the cell middle, not on the surface it describes), **symmetric** (so a floor and the ceiling at the same angle were the same picture — a symmetric mark cannot say which side is solid), **two different colours** (red on the map, blue in the picker, via a duplicate `COLLISION_ANGLE_NEEDLE` constant), and **sized in world px with no density gate**, which is the "scattered noise" at low zoom. The picker additionally routed the angle through `angleDegrees`, whose rounding cost up to half a degree. **The replacement is one mark drawn by one function** (`src/core/collision/collision-angle-mark.ts`) at every call site — both map overlays, the picker thumbnails, the big preview, the paint ghost, and the legend swatch (which now draws the REAL mark with the real code instead of a CSS rule that could drift from it): a **tangent bar anchored ON the surface** (median solid column) with an **outward barb** giving it the asymmetry that finally distinguishes a floor from a ceiling. **The tangent comes from the angle byte; the OPEN SIDE comes from the height sign, not the angle** — deliberately, because a game's convention for a ceiling's stored angle is a per-game question this module refuses to guess at, and the geometry is unambiguous and local, so the choice is right under either convention and cannot double-negate. (Verified: deleting that override leaves the 'y-flip reverses the barb' row still GREEN, because `flipAngleY` happens to do it alone for this table — that row is **non-discriminating** for this property and the 254-byte sweep is what actually holds it.) **Legibility over art is a dark casing stroked under every bright core** — one stroke vanishes on art that matches it, a cased pair contrasts against anything, the same trick that keeps map labels readable over an arbitrary basemap. **Zoom is decided deliberately per quantity:** lengths in cell-local (world) px so the mark stays proportional to the cell it annotates, stroke widths in screen px so a hairline stays a hairline, and no mark at all below `MIN_CELL_PX_FOR_MARK` (14 screen px per 16px cell) — with the legend saying "Angle — zoom in to show" so that suppression is not a trap. **The spare conventions are deleted, not left lying around:** `needleEndpoints` and `COLLISION_ANGLE_NEEDLE` are both gone, so there is one angle direction and one angle colour in the tree. **Two defects were found by looking at rendered output rather than by a test** and are worth recording: a float-noise `ny !== 0` wall test let rounding decide a vertical surface's barb direction (`Math.cos(3π/2)` is -1.8e-16, so $c0 negated and $40 did not), fixed with an epsilon that the 256-byte sweep now pins; and the barb was clipped at the thumbnail border on every full-height shape, fixed with padding rather than by clamping the anchor (a clamp would have moved the mark off the surface, i.e. reintroduced the defect). **Proof:** 5200 vitest passed, plus a CDP harness driving the real app against the owner's own act, reading BOTH `__dbg.aeon.collisionMarks()` — a new PUBLISH written by `OverlayRenderer` out of the exact values it hands to the draw — and the map canvas's own pixels, with the **mirrored-barb position as the discriminating control** (the old symmetric mark painted there; the new one must not). Before/after screenshots of the same curved slope at the same park are in `scratchpad/collision-legibility/`, and the design space including what was rejected is in `docs/reviews/2026-08-28-collision-legibility.md`. **⚠ NOT THE LAST WORD ON THIS MARK — SEE ROW 84.** The owner filed a SECOND report on the same mark hours later, and it was not a regression in anything above: the geometry this row shipped is correct (measured, angle $00 → tangent exactly `(1, 0)`, outward normal exactly `(0, -1)`) and row 84 does not touch it. What row 84 changes is the WEIGHTING this row chose — a prominent tangent bar with a small barb — which spends the mark's ink on the quantity an author is not asking about. Read the two together: this row is why the tangent exists and must not be deleted, row 84 is why it is the quiet element. | M | owner field report 2026-08-28, items 4–6 |
| 74 | ~~**Marquee: select by TILE, preview what you copied, and an undo that actually repaints.**~~ — **DELIVERED 2026-08-28**, branch `feat/marquee-preview-tiles` (`0608bf0` items 2+3, `759b481` item 4). Three owner reports from one session: *"when I select something it doesn't preview what's selected"* (sharpened with a screenshot of the PASTE GHOST — *"when I press control v to paste it later it should have that preview in this empty stamp shape right?"*), *"it should allow me to select by tiles right? Right now it's only block size"*, and *"control + z or undo doesn't work with pasting from marquee"*. **THE MEASUREMENT ITEM 3 TURNS ON, and it is a CONSTRAINT not a bug:** art is per-8px TILE (one nametable word each) but collision is per-16px CELL — `cellTileIndices` returns FOUR tile indices for ONE word, so a rect that does not begin AND end on even tile coords owns no complete cell at that edge. Tile-granular selection is structurally fine for art and structurally impossible for collision. **RULE: a selection carries collision IFF its RECTANGLE is block-aligned**, keyed on geometry and never on the armed mode (a Tile-mode drag landing on even bounds behaves exactly like a Block-mode one), with every surface saying so where it matters — peach marquee stroke, the copy toast, a disabled `Collision` layer button with its reason, and save-as-chunk REFUSING (`chunkCellCount` floors, so a 5x3 chunk would carry two cells describing its top-left 4x2 and stamping it would write air over real collision). An art-only clipboard carries **length-0** planes, not zero-filled ones — a zero-filled plane of the right length is indistinguishable from "all air" and would ERASE the destination's collision — and pastes on the TILE grid, because a selection you can make but cannot place at that precision is worse than one you cannot make. **RULED OUT:** sub-block collision (no representation for half a cell — it would have to invent a winner among four tiles); collision taken from the ENCLOSING block rect (the two halves would then disagree about the footprint and a paste would write collision OUTSIDE the marquee); and both silent degradations the parcel was told not to make. **CONSISTENCY:** the facet already answers tile-vs-block with `paint-tile`/`paint-block`, and NEITHER writes collision — the Snap control restates that vocabulary rather than inventing one. **ITEM 2 OVERTURNS A DOCUMENTED DECISION, on evidence:** the paste ghost was footprint-only because "a paste's contents are what you just copied and still remember" — an empirical claim about the author, contradicted by the author. Its neighbour the stamp ghost already draws real art and argues for it because "which chunk" matters as much as "where"; a clipboard region is MORE arbitrary than one of seventy library chunks, so that argument is stronger for paste. **ITEM 4 was a WORKAROUND MASKING ITS OWN DEFECT:** the invalidation listener's `switch (cmd.type)` had no `'batch'` case, so a paste (a BatchCommand) marked nothing dirty and the section canvas kept the pasted pixels — while the paste and stamp click handlers reached into the batch BY HAND to make the forward edit visible, which the undo path cannot copy (`BoundEditHistory.undo()` is argument-free). Blast radius measured, wider than reported: chunk stamp identically broken and unreported, and ComposerCanvas's multi-tile art edit changed nothing on the map at ALL. Listener now walks batches and **COALESCES** (a naive recursion would call `rebuildTileArt()` — a whole-act re-prerender — once per leaf); both manual reaches DELETED. **`src/renderer/canvas/region-preview.ts` is one module because a fixed 128x128 buffer paired with a natively-sized ImageData threw RangeError and once UNMOUNTED THE REACT ROOT** (the owner's earlier crash); all three ghosts go through it now, and planting the fixed buffer back made the harness report `canvases: []` — the root gone, live. **PROOF:** node 5,204 pass (+32 rows; 1 pre-existing failure, `effects-scene-curve-vsplit`, identical on an unmodified master clone) and `scratchpad/marquee-harness.mjs` **34/34 on three runs**, whole-canvas diffs, real CDP events. Findings: `docs/reviews/2026-08-28-marquee-preview-tiles.md`. **TAGGED for foreground:** chunk-stamp undo repaint and art-facet map repaint are fixed but not runtime-driven. | 76 | ~~**"Is there a way to draw the higher priority and such?" — and under that question, SILENT DATA LOSS: painting a tile destroyed the priority and flip bits of the cell it landed on.**~~ — **DELIVERED 2026-08-28**, branch `feat/tile-attribute-authoring`, packet `docs/reviews/2026-08-28-tile-attributes.md`, instrument `scratchpad/tile-attribute-harness.mjs`, runner `npm run harness:tile-attributes`. **THE ASK CAME FROM ROW 72'S LENS AND THE ANSWER WAS NO** — the owner, having just been given a way to SEE which foreground art draws in front of him, immediately asked for a way to author it. **Looking for that turned up the defect.** `packNametableWord` carries five fields (tileIndex 0..10, hFlip 11, vFlip 12, palette 13..14, priority 15); every interactive paint site wrote exactly two, open-coded as `(tile & 0x7FF) | (pal << 13)`. **FOUR sites, not the two first reported**: paint-tile's press, paint-tile's DRAG (only reachable by a real gesture), paint-block, and the background stroke. So a stroke over a high-priority tile dropped it behind Sonic and a stroke over a flipped tile un-flipped it — invisible for as long as it existed, because until row 72 nothing in this engine could show priority at all. **THE RULE, and it is per-field rather than one of the three obvious answers:** the brush owns the PICTURE, the cell keeps its DEPTH. Flips come from the brush (a flip is which picture the cell shows, and the picker depicts it unflipped, so painting must put down what you saw); priority is TRI-STATE defaulting to `keep`, because depth is a property of the cell and nothing in the picker depicts it. **`keep` beats `off` on DETECTABILITY, not taste:** both defaults have a real failure mode, but keep's (priority carried onto sky) is veiled violet by row 72's lens the moment it happens, while off's is the ABSENCE of a veil, which nobody notices until a player walks behind a bush. **Authoring is chips beside the picker**, in classic's existing vocabulary (`X flip` / `Y flip` / `Priority`) so the two halves of the app do not name one thing two ways; arming a non-default priority brush surfaces the lens and toasts that it did. Rejected: modifier keys (Alt is spent twice over, and an invisible modifier for an invisible field reproduces the property that hid the bug) and a dedicated attribute tool (an eighth tool on a seven-tool facet that still could not lay a tile and its depth in one stroke). ⚠ **THE CLASSIC PATH DOES NOT SHARE THE DEFECT** — its block and chunk composers already pack all five fields and already ship this exact control, which is why the vocabulary was copied rather than invented. ⚠ **THE AGENT `paint-region` PATH IS ADJACENT AND WAS LEFT ALONE**: it defaults omitted `pri`/`hf`/`vf` to false, which is a documented whole-entry spec rather than a truncation, but an agent painting over authored priority still clears it — see the packet. **Proven red-first on the running app: 21/37 on master, 37/37 fixed, three green runs**; 16 node tests over the shared rule, each proven assertive by planting both rejected rules. |

Node suite +11 rows on `camera-preview.test.ts`, red-first at 11/11 with `src/` reverted to master. |

One rule, symmetric, and the author never has to recall which way the panel is set — the modifier is always the other one. So every drag targets an ODD rect (`2b` asserts the oddness, `2d` asserts the two derived expectations actually differ — on even bounds the granularities AGREE and every row would measure nothing), `3c` and `4c` each compare a held drag against the SAME drag unheld, and `5a`/`5b` assert the whole grid: collapsed pairs equal, pairs different. Reverse-only puts every tile in the right PLACE drawn the wrong way round; toggle-only mirrors every tile WHERE IT STANDS. **Neither looks wrong on symmetric art, which is most of a tiled background** — so both halves were PLANTED INTO THE IMPLEMENTATION and measured: node 9/27 and 7/27 red, CDP **32/40** each (`2c: expected [ Array(8) ] to not deeply equal [ Array(8) ]`). `X`/`Y` is Tiled's binding and — decisively — this engine's own vocabulary (`collision-cell-word.ts` names bit 10 `xFlip`, *"mirror horizontally"*; classic's composers already ship `X flip`/`Y flip`). `check_intervals` says the same one level up and names the consequence — two records on a fire line store an arm gap of -1, whose byte is `RASTER_ARM_PARK`, killing every later fire in the frame silently. **So abutting bands DO NOT BUILD**: `{top, L}` beside `{L, bot}` is a refused program, not a tight one. ⚠ **AND THAT IS A DATED CLAIM, CORRECTED 2026-08-30 — IT LANDED HERE STATED AS AN ENGINE LAW AND IT IS NOT ONE.** Verified at aeon `2e976223`: **OVERLAP IS DESIGNED, NOT IMPOSSIBLE** are their own words at the head of `check_intervals`, and a complete, three-times-swept runtime overlap-resolution design (**effects tail Part A**) is BANKED 2026-08-17 by owner ruling, revival condition *“a real program that needs overlapping patchable bands”*. **Owner: aeon's lane, not this one.** Two qualifications keep a reader from waiting for the wrong event: Part A lifts `check_intervals`' wall for **patchable-vs-patchable pairs ONLY** (statics stay sacrosanct) while a preset document lowers to `band()`, which is *“Static by construction … not handable to `patchable`”*; and the ensure an abutting PAIR actually hits is `raster_program`'s restore-alone guard, which Part A does not name. The claim is stated **once**, with its date, owner, expiry and re-read list, in the GAP RULE block of `src/renderer/providers/effects-preset.ts` — this row points there rather than re-deriving it. ⚠ **Nothing in the editor was loosened**: aeon's guard says every naive relaxation was proven unsound, so the refusal stays exactly as strict as it is. A split therefore cuts at L and starts the lower half at **L+1**, leaving L showing the base palette — which is also why a band needs three lines to have one to give, a minimum DERIVED from the two inequalities rather than written down. The band id cannot collide by construction (aeon packs `top * 128 + sa`; the halves share `sa` and differ in `top`). **WHAT BOUNDS AN EDGE — four rules, two KINDS, and the join of two existing repo rulings.** The fire bound (3..223, `fire()`, imported from `effects-aeon.ts` rather than re-typed) and `top < bot` (`band()`) hold whatever else the document says, so the drag **CLAMPS** and says which of the two stopped it (`clampLayerTop` + `guideBoundNotice`'s shape). (2) **Three states, and `0` is a real world Y** — a new channel is born on `(EFFECTS_FIRE_LINE_MIN+EFFECTS_FIRE_LINE_MAX)/2` and never on 0, the `unused` option reads *"channel unused (null)"* with no `0` in it, and the sentinel is refused AS AN INTEGER with the sentence pointing at the other spelling. | 124 | ~~**A GREEN HARNESS REPORTED RED TO EVERY SWEEP THAT READ ITS EXIT CODE — AND IT WAS DELETING ANOTHER WRAPPER'S AUTH FILE TO DO IT.**~~ — **DELIVERED 2026-09-03** (O78), branch `fix/o78-harness-exit-code`, packet `docs/reviews/2026-09-03-o78-harness-exit-code.md`, gate rows **`[o7]`/`[o8]`** in `npm run harness:xvfb-reap`. **The harness's own exit code was never wrong: it exits 0.** Under an OUTER `xvfb-run` — `xvfb-run -a npm run harness:band-preset`, the shape a sweep uses — the harness printed `44 rows, 0 failed`, `NPM_EXIT=0`, and the wrapper exited **1**. **Cause, measured end to end:** `xvfb-run` exports `XAUTHORITY=/tmp/xvfb-run.XXXXXX/Xauthority.…` to the command (`:180`); harnesses build the child env as `{...process.env}` and `delete env.DISPLAY` — **`DISPLAY` goes, `XAUTHORITY` stays** — so every process in the harness's own tree carries the OUTER wrapper's value; `displayArtifacts()` read it out of `/proc/<pid>/environ`, matched `XVFB_TMPDIR_RE`, called the directory ours, and `reapDisplays()` `rm -r`'d it; the wrapper then ran `xauth remove` at `:188` against a file that was gone, and `set -e` (armed `:26`, re-armed `:182`) aborted it **before `exit $RETVAL` at `:197`**. **It is the `DISPLAY` trap one field over** — the docstring above `displayArtifacts` already forbids taking a display number from an inherited env var, and the same reasoning was never applied to `XAUTHORITY`. **The prior record's cause is WRONG and is corrected here:** `harness-red-sweep.md` §8 blamed `kill $XVFBPID` on a departed X server; the outer `Xvfb` (pid 2438930, `:287`) was measured **still running after the harness**, and the failing call is `xauth remove` (replayed by hand: `xauth: error in locking authority file …`, exit 1). **DISCRIMINATOR — does the teardown reach the X reaper with the tree STILL ALIVE?** `await killTree(child)`/`killTreeSync` capture pre-signal by construction → affected; a harness that signals `-child.pid` itself and waits leaves the exit net an empty descent → not affected; a non-launcher never reaches the reaper. That is why `harness:d27-effects-focus` was clean under the same wrapper. **THE 101 IS CORRECTED.** It is O49's count of unregistered instruments (145 tracked − 44 registered, ROADMAP row 115), reused as the O50 sweep's population size (`unregistered_population_of_this_sweep: 101`) — never a count of harnesses hit by this. **On that same 101-row sweep the measured blast radius was 2**: `animated-art` and `chunk-links`, both carrying the sweep's own words *"process exit 1 — teardown noise from the xvfb wrapper, after every row had passed"* (a floor, not a ceiling — a masked green inside its 28 RED / 14 UNRUNNABLE rows would be invisible). **Today: 126 registered `harness:*`, 110 launch an app, 32 tear down with `killTree` (deterministically affected), 78 self-kill, 16 never launch.** SAMPLE, NOT CENSUS — 10 run under the reproducing shape: **4/4 `killTree` callers affected** (`band-preset`, `capture`, `collision-read`, `loop-paint` — all `outer_tmpdir_survives=NO`, all now 0), **6/6 self-killers unaffected**; the other 72 were not measured and are not extrapolated to. **Fix** (`scratchpad/lib/harness-guard.mjs`): new exported `inheritedXauthDirs()` reads `XAUTHORITY` from our own env and every ancestor's environ; `displayArtifacts` no longer claims those dirs and returns them in a new `inherited` field (loud, not silently dropped); **GATE 5** in `reapDisplays` refuses one even when handed it directly. **O20/O65/O66 untouched** — ordered teardown, the `ORDERED` line and gates 1-4 all still pass. **Nothing exits 0 unconditionally**: proved BOTH directions across ALL THREE shapes — `44 rows, 0 failed` → 0/0/0, and row `[6b]` mutated so the run completes at `44 rows, 1 failed` → 1/1/1. Gate red-first with the mutation quoted from disk: `if (false && inheritedDirs.has(dir))` at `:705` turns `[o7]` red **and really deletes the fixture** (`stillOnDisk=false`) while `[o8]` stays green; dropping the `ours.has(dir)` branch at `:641` turns `[o8]` red (`claimedOuter=true`) while `[o7]` stays green — first runs, fixture regenerated per run so no plant eats it. **Pre-existing flake named, not introduced:** `xvfb-reap`'s `[s*]`/`[g1]` rows are load-sensitive — four runs on stashed-clean `68c35aff` gave 19/1, 20/0, 19/1, 20/0. `npm test` 474 files / 6580 tests passed, 2 files + 8 tests skipped, every skip named. |

A new block pins the site: located by `setRampSpanCommand(` rather than by a key name, propless and unguarded, nothing between it and the card's controls that opens a different surface, the total exactly three, and no hand-typed copy of the sentence on the card. |

what ships arms a unique marker before the close and verifies the bytes in the profile's own `Local Storage/leveldb` **after the process is gone** — no wait, no retry (a dead process writes nothing). **36 of 36 sessions across 9 runs now print that the close carried their flush**, at 171-173 s, unchanged. ⚠ **The check is STRICTLY STRONGER than the guard it backs up**: `waitRestored` asserts the key is PRESENT and a stale-but-present value passes it — the negative-control run scored **52/52 with three sessions' last writes lost**. ⚠ **A SECOND DEFECT, FOUND BY THE MEASUREMENT AND WORSE THAN THE FIRST**: `cdp()` settled a request only on a matching `message` and never on `close`/`error`, so a target dying mid-request left a promise unsettled, node's event loop empty, and the **whole run exiting 0 at ~45% with no summary** — a sweep reading exit codes records that as a PASS (the O78 class by another road). Rationale: P2 is the suite's keystone gap — Aurora already *serves* Aether and the outbound
half is what the 2026-07-01 suite audit named — and it builds exactly the plumbing design
#8's live preview needs, which is why it still precedes P5. The classic spine has overtaken
P1's aeon half, so P1's remainder should be re-cut against what facet parity already gave for
free rather than executed as written. P3 and P7 stay pure-Aurora work that can interleave any
time engine work stalls.

**Incoming arc (agreed 2026-08-22 with the aeon overseer): parallax/raster effects authoring view** — multi-band parallax + raster effects + BgAnim tile bands, Aurora-authored (the OJZ BG showcase direction). Sequence: aeon's read-only survey of this repo (pinned at `4cffe45`) → owner design review → aeon's S4LZ-slicing engine parcel → Aurora parcels cut HERE against aeon's committed briefs/contracts (SHAs to be pinged across). Lane split: Aurora's overseer dispatches and lands all Aurora parcels; aeon ships committed briefs; cross-tool contract material goes to empyrean. ~~Known load-bearing caveat handed to their survey: the aeon ProjectAdapter is still a routing marker (§2.5)~~ — **THAT CAVEAT WAS FALSE and Aurora is the source of the error** (corrected 2026-08-22). `open()` has performed the real load since `4782e86` (2026-08-13); `useProject.loadFromPath` no longer exists. The stale §2.5 text propagated into aeon's effects assessment and was refuted there — see `reviews/2026-08-22-aeon-effects-survey-verification.md`. The real gap is narrower: `AeonProjectData` names no scene, preset, band or budget, so that model must be **extended**; it is not a loader that must be built, and any estimate priced as such is wrong.

Watch items from the engine side: `section_id` byte→word (floating origin) — check Aurora's
section keying when it lands; engine/game split (#5) — `project.json` may gain a
game-manifest pointer (the split happened on 2026-07-07: `games/demo` now boots on the
agnostic engine); per-act ROM budget gate (#1) — surface its numbers in the budget UI when
the manifest exists.

## 6. Acceptance bar (per phase)

A phase is done when: tsc + vitest green with new coverage for every format codec;
every new mutation is one undo step and has an agent tool; formats are Zod-validated
with build-gate parity (what the generator rejects, Aurora flags at edit time); the
feature is GUI-verified by the user (Electron; no headless); relevant docs updated
(this file's phase table + MCP.md tool list); and — for generator-coupled work — a
golden test proving Aurora's writer and the Python reader agree byte-for-byte.

Two amendments from the August line (§2.6), both learned the hard way:

- **"an MCP tool" is the wrong phrasing** — one `EDITOR_METHODS` entry in
  `src/main/editor-methods.ts` serves **both** MCP and Aether, so the parity obligation is
  *registry* work: schema entry + `AgentRequest` kind (`src/shared/agent-protocol.ts`) +
  a case in `src/renderer/agent/agent-handler.ts`. There is no MCP-specific half.
- **vitest cannot see React or canvas.** Anything whose behaviour is a rendered surface, a
  mouse gesture or a live store round-trip needs a CDP harness driving the real app
  (`scratchpad/*-harness.mjs` — the collision pair are the reference), run in the
  foreground by the controller. Background agents must never call `mcp__oracle__*`; they
  deadlock. And **plant a violation before believing any guard**: a guard that asserts
  nothing is the dominant defect class here, and a defect planted in the wrong function —
  there are usually two near-identical dispatch lines — passes a full build-and-run cycle
  looking convincing.
